# FnbService

> Generated by `tools/build-service-docs.py`. **Do not edit.**

|  |  |
|---|---|
| Tier | operations: What a venue does with what it sold. Licensed per module. |
| Contracts | `fnb` |
| Schemas owned | `fnb` |
| Operations in the slice | 75 of 135 |
| Scale | Write-heavy during service, idle between. Two peaks a day, sharply. |
| If it is down | Down means the kitchen falls back to paper. Offline-capable by design. |

## Why this is a service

**96 operations and 34 tables — the largest single domain after marketing.** Its own service because a kitchen pass has a latency profile nothing else shares: a bump must land in under a second on a display that may be offline.

## Group: bill

### closeTableVisit

**`POST /table-visits/{visitId}/close`**: Settle and close a visit

Converts the visit into one or more sales orders and posts to the ledger. The table moves to `needsClearing`, not `free` — clearing is a separate act by a separate person.

**An unsplit bill closes as one sub-bill** (Chinmay, 3 October 2026, Block A business rules; CHG-RUL-001). Where `splitBill` was never called, payments carry no `subBillId`: the server makes the one sub-bill for the whole bill and the payments settle it. Where the bill was split, every payment names its sub-bill, and a payment without one is refused 400.

**Payment timing follows the outlet** (`tenancy.Outlet.paymentTiming`, as POS-021; CHG-CSA-010). At a `sendFirst` outlet, the default, the payments here settle the bill. At a `payFirst` outlet each round was charged before it reached the kitchen, so the payments here settle only what is still unpaid, and a visit with nothing outstanding closes with `payments` empty.

| Reads | `cache:idempotency`, `fnb.kitchen_ticket`, `fnb.kitchen_ticket_line`, `fnb.service_order`, `fnb.service_order_line`, `fnb.table_visit` |
| Writes | `cache:idempotency`, `fnb.service_order`, `fnb.table_visit` |
| Called by | EMP-059, POS-028 |
| State model | Table visit ([states/table-visit.yaml](../../../states/table-visit.yaml)): moves `billRequested` -> `settled`, `open` -> `settled` |

### splitBill

**`POST /table-visits/{visitId}/bill/split`**: Split a bill

By amount, by covers, or by category (03 Aug §5). Splitting happens **at close, not at order time** — a split computed while the party is still ordering falls apart the moment someone orders another drink.
Remainders are distributed to the minor unit. A split that does not sum to the total is rejected rather than rounded away.

**Five methods, one request** (Chinmay, 3 October 2026, Block A business rules; CHG-RUL-001): `method` is `byAmount` (`amounts`), `byCovers` (`parts`, default the visit's covers), `byCategory` (`categoryAssignments`), `byLine`, the item split (`lineAssignments`), or `bySeat` (`lineAssignments`, each line going to the part of its `seatNumber`). POS-028 and EMP-059 send this one request with the method the server picked; there is no operation per method. **A bill that is never split closes as one sub-bill**: `closeTableVisit` takes its payments without a `subBillId`.

**Payment timing follows the outlet, as on POS-021** (`tenancy.Outlet.paymentTiming`, CHG-CSA-010; both configurations): at a `sendFirst` outlet, the default, the party orders, the kitchen cooks, and the bill is split and paid at close. At a `payFirst` outlet each round is charged before it reaches the kitchen (`createFnbOrder`), so the split divides only what is still unpaid at close (the bill's lines with no payment against them) and `amounts` sum to that outstanding total.

|  |  |
|---|---|
| Permission | `ORDER_MODIFY` |
| Scope level | venue |
| Part of slice | core |
| Wave | 1 |
| Offline | yes |
| Conflict policy | serverWins |
| Reads | `cache:idempotency`, `fnb.bill_split`, `fnb.sub_bill` |
| Writes | `cache:idempotency`, `fnb.bill_split`, `fnb.sub_bill` |
| Called by | EMP-059, POS-028 |
| State model | SubBill ([states/sub-bill.yaml](../../../states/sub-bill.yaml)): moves `open` -> `voided` |

**Parameters**

| Name | In | Required | Type | Notes |

## Group: fnb

### attachModifierGroup

**`PUT /menu-items/{menuItemId}/modifier-groups`**: Give an item its choices

Board 2E. **Attachment is separate from definition** because that is what makes the group reusable — the same *cooked how* group on nine steaks, defined once.
**Refused where the group adds an allergen the item's claim does not carry.** A modifier that adds cheese to a dish declared dairy-free is a mislabelled dish, and **the refusal belongs here rather than in a training note.**

### buildProductionPlan

**`POST /production-plans`**: Turn a forecast into a prep list

Board 2M. **Demand forecast through recipes to quantities per item and per station.**
**Draft, always.** The plan starts from `requestSuggestion(kind=prepPlan)` and is edited before it is released — **a forecast a chef cannot overrule is a forecast a chef ignores**, and the edit is the signal that trains the next one.
**The suggested quantity is kept beside the planned one**, the same rule as `updateRequisitionLines`: the gap is the evidence the forecast is wrong.

### chaseStation

**`POST /kitchen-stations/{stationId}/chase`**: The pass asks a station where an item is

Board 3. **A ticket waiting on one station while the rest of the table is plated** — the expeditor chases, and until now that happened by shouting.
**Recorded, because a chased station is a signal.** One station chased six times a service is understaffed or badly laid out, and the venue only finds that out if the chase leaves a trace. The trace is a `KitchenException` of kind `chased`.

### createTableReservation

**`POST /table-reservations`**: Book a table in advance

4.9.13. **Distinct from `claimTableSession`**, which identifies a table a guest is already sitting at. This is a booking with a time, a party size and nobody present.
**Does not name a specific table by default.** A host seats a party on the night, and committing table 7 at booking time means every later booking is refused against a constraint that did not need to exist. A table may be named where a guest asked for one.
**Dining tables only** (decided 29 September, rev 3 GAP-C2). A table on a venue map is a `resources.Resource` of kind `table`: a non-dining spot, such as a beach or event table, picked on the map and sold like a cabana (`resources.createResourceHold`, then the order). A restaurant table is booked here, with the waitlist (audit R073 (d)), and is never placed on a venue map as a bookable resource.
**No deposit unless the venue switches one on** (decided 29 September, rev 3 REV3-8b, which supersedes audit R077 (a) "no table deposit in the first release": the capability ships, disabled by default). The venue's `orders.DepositPolicy.dining` says whether a table deposit is held, from what party size, and how much (per guest, per table, or a percentage of a minimum spend); nothing about the amount is fixed in code. **With `dining.enabled` false, the default, or a party below `dining.appliesFromPartySize`, the booking takes no payment and never enters the cart**: it is created `booked` and the guest sees a "no card needed" confirmation straight after this call (decided 29 September, rev 3 REV3-8). A basket line for a free table is prototype-only.
**Where a deposit applies, the booking is created `awaitingDeposit`** and `deposit` carries the amount, the basis and `holdExpiresAt`. The cover is held while the guest pays: the client adds the deposit to the cart with `orders.addCartLine` (`variantId` from `deposit.variantId`, and `tableReservationId`) and pays through `checkoutCart` like any other line. When the payment is authorised the booking moves to `booked`; if `holdExpiresAt` passes first, it is `cancelled` and the cover released (states/table-reservation.yaml). The deposit is an `orders.deposit` row, held and later released, applied to the bill or forfeited under the policy; it is not a sale.

| Conflict policy | serverWins |

| Writes | `cache:idempotency`, `fnb.reservation_table`, `fnb.table_reservation` |
| Called by | GST-070, WEB-036 |
| State model | Table reservation ([states/table-reservation.yaml](../../../states/table-reservation.yaml)): created as `booked` or `awaitingDeposit` |

### createTableReservationForGuest

**`POST /outlets/{outletId}/table-reservations`**: Book a table for a guest (staff)

**A host or cashier books a table on a guest's behalf** (contract gap logged in CHG-WIR-011 for POS-028; built in CHG-CSA-045). The same booking as `createTableReservation` (the same deposit rule, `orders.DepositPolicy.dining`, the same states), taken by staff: the outlet is the one in the path, the guest is named by `subjectId` where known or by `guestName` and `contactPoint`, and the booking records who took it (`TableReservation.takenByPrincipalId`). Where a deposit applies, the booking is created `awaitingDeposit` and the deposit is taken at the till like any other line.
Amending or cancelling a booking from the staff app (EMP-055) is a later-block gap (CHG-CSA-047).

### escalateCorrectiveAction

**`POST /food-safety/corrective-actions/{actionId}/escalate`**: Escalate a finding

Raised above the person who found it — a critical reading, or one nobody actioned in time. **Escalation is not a failure state**: it is how a finding reaches somebody with the authority to sign it.

**An escalation goes to the venue's food-safety lead** (decided 28 September, audit R096 (9)): the principal named in the venue setting `fnb.foodSafetyLeadPrincipalId`. The action's `escalatedToPrincipalId` is set to that person and they are notified. A venue with no lead named refuses the escalation with a `409` (`no-food-safety-lead`), because an escalation to nobody is the finding going quiet.

| Permission | `INCIDENT_MANAGE` |

| Conflict policy | serverWins |
| Reads | `cache:idempotency`, `fnb.corrective_action`, `identity.principal` |
| Writes | `cache:idempotency`, `fnb.corrective_action` |
| Called by | BO-044 |
| State model | CorrectiveAction ([states/corrective-action.yaml](../../../states/corrective-action.yaml)): moves `open` -> `escalated`, `actioned` -> `escalated` |

### fireCourse

**`POST /kitchen-tickets/{ticketId}/fire`**: Send a held course to the pass

Board 3 of the client F&B pack. **`KitchenTicket.coursing` carried the policy — `holdAndFire`, `phased`, `timed` — and nothing fired anything.** A venue could configure how coursing works and never course a single table.
**Starters before mains is the entire job of a kitchen pass**, and firing is the act that makes it happen: the server judges the table is ready and the kitchen starts the next course.
**Offline-capable and it must be.** The pass is the one screen that cannot wait for a network, and a fire that queues is a fire that happened.
**Table service only.** Coursing belongs to a table visit, which pays at close whatever the outlet's payment timing; a `payFirst` outlet's order (`tenancy.Outlet.paymentTiming`, CHG-CSA-010) reaches the kitchen when it is paid, never by a fire.

| Writes | `cache:idempotency`, `fnb.kitchen_ticket` |
| Called by | EMP-058, KIT-003 |

### holdCourse

**`POST /kitchen-tickets/{ticketId}/hold`**: Stop a course going out

Board 3. **The other half of firing, and the one that gets forgotten.** A table that has gone quiet, a guest stepped outside, a course that arrived early — the kitchen needs to hold, and a pass with fire and no hold pushes food at a table that is not ready.
**A held course does not lose its place.** It keeps its position in the rail so the kitchen can see how long it has been waiting, because **a held course nobody fires becomes a cold course nobody wants.**

### joinRestaurantWaitlist

**`POST /waitlist`**: Add a party to an outlet's waitlist

BL-130. **Distinct from `queue`, which is for rides.** A restaurant waitlist has a party size, a seating preference and a walk-away point.
**A guest who leaves is not a guest who was served**, and `walkedAway` exists so that distinction survives into the report a manager reads about wait times.
**Joining takes no payment and never enters the cart** (decided 29 September, rev 3 REV3-8): the guest sees the confirmation straight after this call. A waitlist entry never holds a deposit, even where the venue's `DepositPolicy.dining` is on, because it is not a promise of a table.

### leaveRestaurantWaitlist

**`POST /waitlist/{entryId}/leave`**: Take a party off an outlet's waitlist

**A guest could join a restaurant waitlist and not leave it** (decided 28 September, audit R073 (d)). A party that has gone elsewhere and cannot say so is a table held for nobody, and a `noShow` recorded against a guest who told us.
**The entry ends `cancelled`, from `waiting` or `notified`** (states/restaurant-waitlist.yaml). Leaving after being called releases the held table at once rather than at `holdExpiresAt`. `walkedAway` is not this: that is a host recording a party that left without saying so.
**A guest leaves only their own entry** (`subjectId` is the caller); a host may take any party off the list with `ORDER_MODIFY`. Idempotent: leaving an entry that is already `cancelled` returns it unchanged.

| Writes | `fnb.waitlist_entry` |

| State model | Restaurant waitlist entry ([states/restaurant-waitlist.yaml](../../../states/restaurant-waitlist.yaml)): moves `waiting` -> `cancelled`, `notified` -> `cancelled` |

### listMyTableReservations

**`GET /me/table-reservations`**: The guest's own table bookings and waitlist places

**What GST-070 lists under "Your reservations"** (Chinmay, 3 October 2026, Block A business rules; CHG-RUL-013). The caller's own table reservations (`createTableReservation`) and restaurant waitlist entries (`joinRestaurantWaitlist`), across the venues of the tenant. **Ordered by** the booking's time, soonest first (a waitlist entry by when it was joined), the row's id as the tiebreak. **Self-scoped**: the subject is the session's, never a parameter, so the list cannot be asked for anybody else; `listTableReservations` stays the host's list under `ORDER_MODIFY`. 5.1.1. **The bookings for a service period.** A host needs to know what is booked and what is actually free, and those differ all evening — the live table state is `getTableMap` on the same outlet, read alongside this. ### logColdChain

**`POST /food-safety/cold-chain`**: The temperature a delivery arrived at, and what was decided

Board 5G. **A delivery arriving warm is a rejection decision made at the door**, and `createGoodsReceipt` had nowhere to record the temperature.
**Taken before the receipt is posted.** Once stock is received it has entered the kitchen, and **a claim against a supplier needs the reading that refused it** rather than one taken afterwards.
**A breach opens a corrective action at `major`** (high), the same as an out-of-range reading (decided 28 September, audit R125 (5)).

| Conflict policy | append |

| Writes | `cache:idempotency`, `fnb.cold_chain_event`, `fnb.corrective_action` |
| Called by | EMP-065 |

### logKitchenException

**`POST /kitchen-exceptions`**: Something went wrong that is not a refire

Board 3. **Equipment down, an item run out mid-ticket, a delivery late, a station short.** `refireItem` covers a dish; this covers everything that costs the kitchen a service and leaves no other trace.
**The reasons are the value.** A venue looking at a bad Saturday needs to know the fryer was down for forty minutes, and that fact currently lives in somebody's memory.

### logTemperature

**`POST /food-safety/temperature-logs`**: Record a temperature check, in range or not

Board 5J. **HACCP records are a UAE regulatory obligation and nothing in the package touched them.**
**An out-of-range reading is recorded, not refused.** It opens a corrective action and keeps the value — **deleting a bad reading is the one thing an inspector looks for**, and an operation that rejects the reading teaches a kitchen to stop taking it.
**Severity is set by what happened** (decided 28 September, audit R125 (5)). An out-of-range reading opens the corrective action at `major` (high). A reading not taken opens one at `minor` (medium), with `source: missedCheck`: the server raises it when a checkpoint's `checkFrequencyMinutes` passes with no log.
**Offline-capable, deliberately.** A walk-in freezer is where the signal is worst and the readings matter most, and a check nobody can record is a check nobody takes.

|  |  |

| Permission | `INCIDENT_REPORT` |
| Scope level | venue |
| Part of slice | setup, makes `fnb.corrective_action`, `fnb.temperature_log` non-empty |
| Wave | 1 |
| Offline | yes |
| Conflict policy | append |
| Reads | `cache:idempotency`, `fnb.corrective_action`, `fnb.temperature_log`, `platform.scope` |
| Writes | `cache:idempotency`, `fnb.corrective_action`, `fnb.temperature_log` |
| Called by | EMP-067 |

### markOrderCollected

**`POST /orders/{orderId}/collected`**: The guest took it

Board 3. **`recordOrderHandover` exists for a staff handover; a collection counter is the guest taking it themselves**, and the two close the ticket for different reasons.
**Closes the fulfilment clock.** Order to ready is the kitchen's number; ready to collected is the venue's, and a counter with food sitting under a lamp for nine minutes is a problem neither number shows on its own.

### notifyServer

**`POST /table-visits/{visitId}/notify-server`**: The kitchen calls the server to the pass

Board 3 and Board 4. **Food ready and nobody collecting it is the commonest reason a plate goes out cold**, and the pass had no way to say so.
**Reaches the server assigned to the table, not the room.** A broadcast to every handheld is one everybody ignores by the second service.
**Where no server is assigned, it goes to the outlet's supervisor on duty** (decided 28 September, audit R125 (6)), never to the room and never to nobody.

### publishMenu

**`POST /menus/{menuId}/publish`**: Make the draft live, now or on a date

Board 2. **`updateMenu` existed, so a menu edited was a menu live** — no draft, no schedule, no rollback. **A venue changing prices for next Monday had to do it on Monday.**
**Publishing is versioned and the previous version stays readable**, which is what makes rollback possible — and a price change that cannot be undone is a price change nobody makes on a Friday. Each publish writes a `MenuVersion`; `listMenuVersions` reads them back, and a publish with an `effectiveAt` also writes the `MenuSchedule` that `listMenuSchedules` shows as pending.
**A scheduled publish takes effect at the outlet's local midnight**, not the tenant's. A venue in Dubai and one in London do not change menu at the same instant. The outlet's zone is its Region's time zone (`RegionSettings.timeZone`); outlets have no zone of their own.

### recallKitchenTicket

**`POST /kitchen-tickets/{ticketId}/recall`**: Bring back a ticket that was bumped by mistake

Board 3. **A bumped ticket disappears from the rail**, and a bump is a single tap on a screen operated by somebody with their hands full.
**The most-used undo in a kitchen**, and the package had none — the only recovery was to re-key the order, which changes its number and loses its age.
**Bounded to the current service.** A recall is for the last few minutes; a ticket from yesterday is not a mis-bump.
**The window is `VenueSettings.fnb.recallWindowMinutes`, counted from the bump**, a venue setting with a tenant default (decided 28 September, audit R094), and never past the end of the current service. **Proposed default 10 minutes, client to correct (audit R094).**

### refireItem

**`POST /kitchen-tickets/{ticketId}/refire`**: Make it again

Board 3. **A refire is not a new order and it must not be.** It is the same line made twice, and a kitchen that records it as a second order sells a dish it gave away.
**Always reasoned**, because the reasons are the data: a station refiring for `overcooked` three times a service is a station with a problem, and one refiring for `guestChangedMind` is a service issue somewhere else.
**The original line stays.** Food cost counts both; the bill counts one.

### releaseProductionPlan

**`POST /production-plans/{planId}/release`**: Make the plan real

Board 2M. **A plan becomes production runs, grouped by station** (decided 28 September, audit R125 (7)). Each line becomes a run of its recipe carrying the line's `stationId`, and the kitchen works from one prep list per station rather than one task per line; lines with no station form one group of their own.
**Separate from building it because a plan is edited before it is released.** A plan that creates runs as it is drafted creates runs nobody asked for, and a kitchen that finds yesterday's abandoned draft in today's prep list stops trusting the list.
**Releasing calls `recordSuggestionOutcome`; it does not write the outcome itself.** ADR-0020: only the AI contract writes AI tables — **the fourth time that boundary has caught a write in this package**, and every time the operation looked reasonable.
The label matters: planned quantities against the forecast are what make a model possible later, and the record has to exist for that to be true.

### rollbackMenu

**`POST /menus/{menuId}/rollback`**: Put the previous version back

Board 2. **The reason to version a menu at all.** A price published wrong at 11am on a Saturday needs one action, not a re-edit of forty items under pressure.
**Rolls forward, never backwards.** The restored version becomes a new version rather than deleting the bad one — **a menu history that can be edited cannot answer *what were we charging at noon*, which is the question a refund dispute asks.**

| Permission | `PRODUCT_CONFIGURE` |

| Part of slice | setup, makes `fnb.menu` non-empty |
| Wave | 1 |
| Offline | no |
| Conflict policy | serverWins |
| Reads | `cache:idempotency`, `fnb.menu`, `fnb.menu_item`, `fnb.menu_section`, `fnb.menu_version` |
| Writes | `cache:idempotency`, `cache:resolution`, `fnb.menu` |
| Called by | BO-045 |

| Idempotency-Key | header | yes | string (uuid) | Client-generated UUIDv7. |

### scheduleMenuPublish

**`POST /menus/{menuId}/schedule`**: Publish it on a date, not now

Board 2J. **`publishMenu` took an `effectiveAt` and nothing listed or cancelled what was pending.** A venue with three scheduled price changes had no way to see them. Pending schedules are now read with `listMenuSchedules`, and `cancelScheduleId` cancels one of them.
**One schedule per menu per date.** A second schedule for a date that already has one is refused with the existing schedule named (flow F31) — cancel it first.
**Takes effect at the outlet's local midnight**, not the tenant's — a venue in Dubai and one in London do not change menu at the same instant. The outlet's zone is its Region's time zone (`RegionSettings.timeZone`); outlets have no zone of their own.

|  |  |

| Permission | `PRODUCT_CONFIGURE` |
| Scope level | venue |
| Part of slice | setup, makes `fnb.menu` non-empty |
| Wave | 1 |
| Offline | no |
| Config scope | outlet |
| Conflict policy | serverWins |
| Reads | `cache:idempotency`, `fnb.menu`, `fnb.menu_schedule` |
| Writes | `cache:idempotency`, `cache:resolution`, `fnb.menu` |
| Called by | BO-045 |

**Parameters**

| Name | In | Required | Type | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| menuId | path | yes | string (uuid) |  |
| Idempotency-Key | header | yes | string (uuid) | Client-generated UUIDv7. |

**Request body**

| Field | Type | Required | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| effectiveAt | string (date-time) |  | Local midnight, in the Region's time zone, of the day the menu goes live, sent as a UTC instant like every date-time (for a Dubai outlet going live on 1 March, 2026-02-28T20:00:00Z). |
| cancelScheduleId | string (uuid) |  | A pending schedule from listMenuSchedules. (nullable) |

| scopePath | string |  | The partition key (ADR-0005). (read-only) |

### seatTableReservation

**`POST /table-reservations/{reservationId}/seat`**: The party arrived and has been sat down

**Where the booking becomes a visit.** The host names the actual table now, and a table session opens against it — which is where a guest claiming the table (`claimLocationSession`) joins this visit rather than starting a second.
**Seats from `booked`, `confirmed` or `noShow` only** (states/table-reservation.yaml). A seated, completed or cancelled booking is refused with its current status.
A party seated at a different size from the booking is recorded as such. A booking for six that arrives as four is a cover a restaurant could have sold.

|  |  |
|---|---|
| Permission | `ORDER_MODIFY` |
| Scope level | venue |
| Part of slice | core |
| Wave | 1 |
| Offline | yes |
| Conflict policy | lastWriterWins |

| Writes | `cache:idempotency`, `fnb.table_reservation`, `fnb.table_session`, `fnb.table_visit` |
| Called by | EMP-058, POS-028 |
| State model | Table reservation ([states/table-reservation.yaml](../../../states/table-reservation.yaml)): moves `booked` -> `seated`, `confirmed` -> `seated`, `noShow` -> `seated` |

| Name | In | Required | Type | Notes |

### setCourseRules

**`PUT /outlets/{outletId}/course-rules`**: How this outlet courses by default

Board 3. **Coursing was a per-ticket field with no default**, so every table was coursed by hand or not at all.
**A fine-dining room and a food court do not course the same way**, and neither should have to be told twice a night.

### setKitchenSla

**`PUT /outlets/{outletId}/kitchen-sla`**: How long a ticket may sit before it is late

Board 3. **`getKitchenSla` was drawn on the board and nothing set the target.** A rail that colours tickets red needs a number, and the package had none.
**Per service mode, deliberately.** A delivery order and a dine-in main do not share a target, and one number for both makes the rail either permanently red or useless.
**`setPriorityWeights` sits here too**: what pushes a ticket up the rail — age, promise time (`KitchenTicket.targetReadyAt`), table stage, a VIP marker — and in what proportion. The weights are what `listKitchenTickets` orders the rail by.

### setTableCombinations

**`PUT /outlets/{outletId}/table-combinations`**: Which tables can be pushed together, and to what capacity

Board 4. **A party of nine at a restaurant of four-tops is the everyday case**, and the package had no way to say that tables 6 and 7 make a nine.
**Declared rather than inferred.** Two adjacent tables do not always combine — a pillar, a step, a service run. **A host knows which pairs work and a floor plan does not.**
Without it, `getTableMap` reports a full room while the venue turns away a booking it could seat.

| Offline | no |
| Config scope | outlet |
| Conflict policy | serverWins |

| Writes | `cache:idempotency`, `cache:resolution`, `fnb.dining_table`, `fnb.table_combination` |
| Called by | EMP-053, POS-024 |

### signCorrectiveAction

**`POST /food-safety/corrective-actions/{actionId}/sign`**: Say what was done, and put a name to it

Board 5J. **The signature is the record.** *Discarded and reset* with nobody against it is not a corrective action, and an inspector reading an unsigned entry treats it as an entry that did not happen.
**A critical finding cannot be signed by the person who raised it.** Escalation exists so a supervisor signs what a cook should not — and the refusal is in the model rather than in a policy document.

| Part of slice | setup, makes `fnb.corrective_action` non-empty |
| Wave | 1 |
| Offline | no |
| Conflict policy | serverWins |
| Reads | `cache:idempotency`, `fnb.corrective_action`, `identity.principal` |
| Writes | `cache:idempotency`, `fnb.corrective_action` |
| Called by | BO-044, EMP-067 |
| State model | CorrectiveAction ([states/corrective-action.yaml](../../../states/corrective-action.yaml)): moves `actioned` -> `signed`, `escalated` -> `signed` |

| Name | In | Required | Type | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| actionId | path | yes | string (uuid) |  |
| Idempotency-Key | header | yes | string (uuid) | Client-generated UUIDv7. |

**Request body**

|---|---|---|---|

| actionTaken | string |  | (nullable) |

### transferTableVisit

**`POST /table-visits/{visitId}/transfer`**: Move a check to another server

BL-125. **A shift change mid-service leaves a check with a server who has gone home.**
**"Change server" on the till (POS-028) in Block A** (Chinmay, 2 October, workbook Q68; CHG-CSA-013).
**The transfer is recorded, not silent** — tips and sales attribution follow the server, and a check that changes hands without a record is a dispute at cash-up.

### updateTableReservation

**`PATCH /table-reservations/{reservationId}`**: Change or cancel a booking

Party size, time, or cancelled. **A reduced party size releases cover immediately** — four seats returned at 18:00 can be sold for the same evening.
**Cancelling is free where no deposit is held** (decided 29 September, rev 3 REV3-8b, superseding audit R077 (a)). Where the venue's `DepositPolicy.dining` took a deposit, cancelling at least `dining.refundableUntilHours` before `startsAt` releases it in full; later, or a `noShow`, keeps it as `dining.onLateCancelOrNoShow` says. A party size change re-prices the deposit only where the basis is per guest: a larger party is asked for the difference, a smaller one has the excess released. Cancelling an `awaitingDeposit` booking releases the cover and nothing is charged.

| Part of slice | core |
| Wave | 1 |
| Offline | no |
| Conflict policy | serverWins |

| Writes | `cache:idempotency`, `fnb.reservation_table`, `fnb.table_reservation` |
| Called by | GST-070, WEB-031, WEB-036 |
| State model | Table reservation ([states/table-reservation.yaml](../../../states/table-reservation.yaml)): moves `awaitingDeposit` -> `cancelled`, `booked` -> `confirmed`, `booked` -> `cancelled`, `confirmed` -> `cancelled` |

## Group: guestOrdering

### claimLocationSession

**`POST /location-sessions`**: Tell the platform where the guest is

Scanning the code on a table, a cabana, a sunbed, or entering a seat number. One operation for all of them — a guest at a poolside and a guest in an auditorium are doing the same thing, and a separate endpoint per furniture type would be four ways to say the same sentence.
**A claim is not a booking.** It attaches the guest to a location so an order can be routed there. Where a table visit is already open the guest joins it rather than starting a second, and both order onto one bill.
Codes rotate. A static code photographed once lets someone order to a cabana they are not at, from outside the venue.

|  |  |

| Permission | `None` |
| Scope level | venue |
| Part of slice | core |
| Wave | 1 |
| Offline | no |
| Conflict policy | serverWins |
| Reads | `cache:idempotency`, `fnb.location_session` |
| Writes | `cache:idempotency`, `fnb.location_session` |
| Called by | GST-024, WEB-036 |

**Parameters**

|---|---|---|---|---|
| Idempotency-Key | header | yes | string (uuid) | Client-generated UUIDv7. |

**Request body**

|---|---|---|---|
| locationCode | string |  | From the QR or NFC tag. |
| seatReference | object |  | For seated venues where the seat is the address and there is no code to scan. |
| seatReference.performanceId | string (uuid) |  |  |
| seatReference.seatId | string |  |  |
| partySize | integer |  | (min 1) |

**Response**: `LocationSession`

| label | string | yes |  |
| outletId | string (uuid) |  | The outlet serving this location. (nullable) |
| visitId | string (uuid) |  | The table visit this session orders onto, where the location is a table. (nullable) |
| joinedExistingVisit | boolean |  |  |
| subjectId | string (uuid) |  |  |
| expiresAt | string (date-time) | yes | Sessions expire so a guest who leaves cannot order to a lounger now occupied by someone else. |

**Responses**

| Code | Shape | Meaning |
|---|---|---|

| 400 |  | Neither a location code nor a seat reference supplied |
| 409 |  | Code expired or unknown, the location is out of service, or no outlet currently delivers to it — a cabana is useless as an address if nothing serves it. |
| 429 | TooManyRequests | Rate limit exceeded for this tenant, venue or principal. |

### createDeliveryLocation

**`POST /delivery-locations`**: Add a place food can be delivered to

### createGuestFnbOrder

**`POST /guest-orders`**: A guest orders food

Order to a table, or for collection from a counter.
**Distinct from `createFnbOrder`, which is staff-operated.** A server placing an order is trusted with an outlet, a table and a price. A guest is trusted with none of those: the outlet comes from the location session (`claimLocationSession`), the price is recomputed server-side and the quoted total is checked against it.
**An order that breaks the outlet's `FnbDeliveryPolicy` is refused with 422**, naming the rule it broke; a 409 is a state that changed under the guest.
Payment is required before the kitchen sees it, unless the outlet runs a tab — a kitchen that cooks on an unpaid guest order is a kitchen giving food away.
**F&B keeps its own payment step and its own receipt** (Chinmay, 2 October, workbook Q52 and Q71; CHG-CSA-011). A guest F&B order does not go through the shared cart (`orders.addCartLine`, `checkoutCart`): it is paid in its own pay step after this call (F11 step 4) and gets its own F&B receipt. DI-293's one cart, one receipt does not apply to F&B.
**Inside the venue, a ticket is needed; a standalone outlet sells without one** (Chinmay, 2 October, workbook Q70; DI-292 amended for standalone outlets; CHG-CSA-011). Where the outlet sits is `tenancy.Outlet.admissionContext` (`OutletAdmissionContext`; "siting" and common `OutletSiting` are its deprecated aliases, CHG-CLN-008), shown to the guest as `DiningOutlet.admissionContext`. At an `insideVenue` outlet the guest must hold a valid admission ticket for the venue for the day, checked through Access (ADR-0068); without one the order is refused `403 entry-ticket-required` and nothing is held. A `standalone` outlet takes takeaway and delivery orders with no ticket.

| Offline | no |

| Writes | `cache:idempotency`, `fnb.kitchen_ticket`, `fnb.order_fulfilment`, `fnb.service_order`, `platform.outbox` |
| Called by | GST-024, GST-032, KSK-016, WEB-036 |
| State model | F&B order ([states/fnb-order.yaml](../../../states/fnb-order.yaml)): created as `ordered` |

### getGuestMenu

**`GET /outlets/{outletId}/guest-menu`**: The menu a guest sees

**Staff since 1 October 2026**: the till's Food & Drink sale (POS-021) shows the same menu in force that a guest sees; the declaration said guest only while P04 called it. A read with no permission and no subject scope, so the audience is the only change.
The menu in force **at this moment** — breakfast at nine, lunch at one. The staff endpoint returns every menu with its availability window; this returns the one that applies, because a guest does not want to work out which.
Unavailable items are returned marked, not removed. A guest who saw a dish yesterday and cannot find it today assumes the app is broken; "sold out" is an answer.
Allergens are always present. Omitting them is not an option a tenant gets to choose.

### listBookableOutlets

**`GET /venues/{venueId}/bookable-outlets`**: The restaurants a guest can book a table at

**The published list GST-070 Reserve a Table picks from** (Chinmay, 3 October 2026, Block A business rules; CHG-RUL-013). Different from `listDiningOutlets`, which answers *where can I eat right now* and drops an outlet that is closed: a booking is for another time, so an outlet is listed whether or not it is open now. **Published data only**: an outlet is listed when it is active, takes table reservations (it has tables, `setTableLayout`, and its reservation policy, `getFnbReservationPolicy`, is active) and is on the venue's published guest app. A draft outlet, or one with no tables, is never listed. Ordered by the outlet's display order, then `name`. Readable before sign-in: a guest chooses a restaurant and signs in to book (`createTableReservation`).

### listDeliveryLocations

**`GET /venues/{venueId}/delivery-locations`**: Where an order can be delivered

4.6.26 — tables, seats, cabanas or designated locations. A venue runs several kinds at once: a restaurant with tables, a poolside with cabanas and sunbeds, an auditorium where the seat number is the address, and a set of named collection points.
They are one concept because a runner needs one instruction, and a guest needs one way to say where they are.
**Staff read it too** (contract gap logged in CHG-WIR-011 for BO-065; CHG-CSA-045): the venue configuration screen lists the locations it creates and edits (`createDeliveryLocation`, `updateDeliveryLocation`). A staff token was already accepted; the audience now says so.

### listDiningOutlets

**`GET /venues/{venueId}/dining`**: Where a guest can eat, right now

The dining list in the guest app. Returns outlets that are **open now**, not every outlet configured — a guest shown a restaurant that closed an hour ago walks there.
Ordering method is stated per outlet, because it varies within one venue: a table service restaurant, a counter that takes app orders for collection, and a bar that does neither are all normal.

### recordOrderHandover

**`POST /guest-orders/{orderId}/delivery`**: Record that an order reached the guest

The closing state, and it differs by fulfilment: a server marks `served`, a counter marks `collected`, a runner marks `delivered`. 4.6.35 distinguishes all three, and so does this — "the guest has it" is not one event when the dispute is about which.
Offline-capable. A runner crossing a venue loses signal, and an order that cannot be closed until they return is an order that reads as undelivered for ten minutes.
**Which outcome closes which service mode** (decided 28 September, audit R125 (1)): `tableService` closes `served`; `quickService` and `collection` close `collected`; `delivery` and `roomService` close `delivered`, and a `delivered` outcome needs `deliveredToLocationId`. Any other pairing is refused `422` with `outcomeNotForServiceMode`, and a delivery with no location with `locationNotSupplied`. `guestNotFound` and `refused` are recorded for every mode.

|  |  |
|---|---|
| Permission | `ORDER_MODIFY` |
| Scope level | venue |
| Part of slice | core |
| Wave | 1 |
| Offline | yes |
| Conflict policy | append |
| Reads | `cache:idempotency`, `fnb.delivery_location`, `fnb.delivery_location_outlet`, `fnb.service_order` |
| Writes | `cache:idempotency`, `fnb.service_order` |
| Called by | BO-021, KIT-006, POS-012 |
| State model | F&B order ([states/fnb-order.yaml](../../../states/fnb-order.yaml)): moves `ready` -> `served`, `ready` -> `collected`, `ready` -> `delivered`<br/>Kitchen ticket ([states/kitchen-ticket.yaml](../../../states/kitchen-ticket.yaml)): moves `ready` -> `served` |

**Parameters**

| Name | In | Required | Type | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| orderId | path | yes | string (uuid) |  |
| Idempotency-Key | header | yes | string (uuid) | Client-generated UUIDv7. |

**Request body**

| Field | Type | Required | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| outcome | enum (served, collected, delivered, guestNotFound, refused) | yes | served, collected and delivered move the order to the FnbOrderStatus of the same name. |
| deliveredToLocationId | string (uuid) |  | Required where outcome is delivered (audit R125 (1)). |
| runnerPrincipalId | string (uuid) |  |  |
| note | string |  | Required for guestNotFound and refused. (max length 500) |
| recordedAt | string (date-time) | yes |  |

**Response**: `GuestOrderStatus`

| Field | Type | Required | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| orderId | string | yes |  |
| orderNumber | string |  |  |
| status | FnbOrderStatus: enum (ordered, accepted, inPreparation, ready, served, collected, delivered, cancelled, …) | yes | The full lifecycle from 4.6.35. |
| estimatedReadyAt | string (date-time) |  | (nullable) |
| isReadyForCollection | boolean |  |  |
| lines | array of object | yes | Per-line status. |
| lines[].name | string |  |  |
| lines[].quantity | integer |  |  |
| lines[].status | KitchenTicketStatus: enum (received, preparing, ready, served, recalled, cancelled) |  |  |

**Responses**

### setDeliveryLocationOutletMapping

**`PUT /venues/{venueId}/delivery-location-outlets`**: Set which outlets deliver to which delivery locations

4.6.26. **The join `listDeliveryLocations` reads and nothing wrote.** Each entry names a delivery location and the outlets that deliver to it; the set given replaces that location's serving outlets, and locations not named are left as they are. An empty `servingOutletIds` means nothing serves the location, which `listDeliveryLocations` then reports as not serviceable rather than hiding. Every outlet must be an F&B outlet of the same venue as the location.

### setFnbDeliveryPolicy

**`PUT /fnb-delivery-policy`**: Set takeaway and delivery rules

**Design gap, 23 September.** The dining design offers takeaway with a collection time and delivery to an address with a delivery window, a minimum order, a fee, free delivery above a threshold and a radius. **The design only displays those rules; this makes them enforceable**, and `createGuestFnbOrder` refuses an order that breaks them. Delivery before this was to places inside the venue only (`listDeliveryLocations`).
**Keyed on `outletId`, one policy per outlet.** The body replaces that outlet's policy, and creates it where the outlet has none — there is no separate create.

### listKitchenTickets

**`GET /kitchen/tickets`**: Kitchen ticket queue

The rail. Read by P15 Kitchen Display — TICVAI's own kitchen display on commodity hardware (19 Sep; the 31 Jul *KDS integration only* position is superseded) — and by the back-office and till views of the same queue.
**Rail order is the server's.** Tickets come back ordered by `priority` and the outlet's priority weights (`setKitchenSla`: age, promise time — `targetReadyAt` —, table stage, VIP), and a display renders them in the order returned rather than re-sorting by arrival or by promise time.
**A kitchen display does not choose its station** (decided 28 September, audit R277). Where the caller is a workstation named in a station's `displayWorkstationIds` and sends no `stationId`, the station resolves from that assignment; a `stationId` sent by an assigned display is ignored. The station-load tile is not in the first release.

### setKitchenTicketStatus

**`PUT /kitchen/tickets/{ticketId}/status`**: Advance a kitchen ticket

Called by P15 Kitchen Display, or the back-office and till views of the rail. Status flows back to the guest app order tracker.
**Four moves only** (states/kitchen-ticket.yaml): `received` to `preparing`, `preparing` to `ready`, `ready` to `recalled`, `recalled` to `preparing`. `served` is reached through `recordOrderHandover` and `cancelled` through `cancelFnbOrder`; any other move is refused with the current status named.

|  |  |
|---|---|
| Permission | `ORDER_MODIFY` |
| Scope level | venue |
| Part of slice | core |
| Wave | 1 |
| Offline | yes |
| Conflict policy | append |
| Reads | `cache:idempotency`, `fnb.kitchen_ticket`, `fnb.kitchen_ticket_line` |
| Writes | `cache:idempotency`, `fnb.kitchen_ticket`, `platform.outbox` |
| Called by | BO-020, BO-046, KIT-002, KIT-003, KIT-006, POS-022 |
| State model | F&B order ([states/fnb-order.yaml](../../../states/fnb-order.yaml)): moves `accepted` -> `inPreparation`, `inPreparation` -> `ready`<br/>Kitchen ticket ([states/kitchen-ticket.yaml](../../../states/kitchen-ticket.yaml)): moves `received` -> `preparing`, `preparing` -> `ready`, `ready` -> `recalled`, `recalled` -> `preparing` |

**Parameters**

| Name | In | Required | Type | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| ticketId | path | yes | string (uuid) |  |
| Idempotency-Key | header | yes | string (uuid) | Client-generated UUIDv7. |

**Request body**

| Field | Type | Required | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| status | KitchenTicketStatus: enum (received, preparing, ready, served, recalled, cancelled) | yes |  |
| lineIds | array of string (uuid) |  | Advance specific lines. |
| stationId | string (uuid) |  |  |
| recordedAt | string (date-time) | yes |  |

**Response**: `KitchenTicket`

| Field | Type | Required | Notes |

### setItemAvailability

**`PUT /menu-items/{itemId}/availability`**: Mark an item available or eighty-sixed

The most-used endpoint in a live kitchen. A sold-out item must disappear from every terminal in the outlet immediately — this does not wait for a bundle publish.
**Immediate, not on save** (decided 28 September, audit R110 (c)). The change is live on every terminal and guest menu of the outlet when this returns; there is no pending state behind a *Save changes* button.

|  |  |
|---|---|
| Permission | `PRODUCT_CONFIGURE` |
| Scope level | venue |
| Part of slice | core |
| Wave | 1 |
| Offline | yes |
| Conflict policy | serverWins |
| Reads | `cache:idempotency`, `fnb.menu_item` |
| Writes | `cache:idempotency`, `fnb.menu_item` |
| Called by | BO-140, KIT-008, POS-021, POS-024 |

**Parameters**

| Name | In | Required | Type | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| itemId | path | yes | string (uuid) |  |
| If-Match | header |  | string | Optimistic concurrency for serverWins (system-design review SD-013, 29 September; ADR-0031). |
| Idempotency-Key | header | yes | string (uuid) | Client-generated UUIDv7. |

**Request body**

| Field | Type | Required | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| isAvailable | boolean | yes |  |
| reason | enum (soldOut, ingredientUnavailable, equipmentDown, seasonal, other) |  | other is allowed only with a note, which it then requires (decided 28 September, audit R222). |
| note | string |  | Free text. (max length 500; nullable) |
| restoreAt | string (date-time) |  | Automatic restore, typically at next service. |
| recordedAt | string (date-time) | yes | Device time of the act (offline-capable; replayed in this order). |

**Response**: `MenuItem`

| Field | Type | Required | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| id | string (uuid) | yes |  |
| productVariantId | string (uuid) | yes | The catalogue variant this item links to, for reporting, stock and tax class only. |
| name | string | yes |  |
| description | string |  | (nullable) |
| price | Money | yes | On the wire this is three fields; in the database it is one column. |
| price.amount | string | yes | Decimal string, never a float. (pattern ^-?\d+(\.\d{1,4})?$) |
| price.currency | string | yes | Resolved from the region, not stored on the row (ADR-0018). (pattern ^[A-Z]{3}$) |
| price.scale | integer | yes | Resolved from the region alongside currency. (min 0; max 4) |
| dailyCount | integer |  | How many portions the kitchen set for today (setMenuItemDailyCount; Chinmay, 2 October, workbook Q194; CHG-CSA-017). (min 0; read-only; nullable) |
| remainingCount | integer |  | What is left of dailyCount ("6 left" on the till and the guest menu). (min 0; read-only; nullable) |
| sortOrder | integer |  |  |
| modifierGroupIds | array of string (uuid) |  |  |
| stationId | string (uuid) |  | (nullable) |
| menuSectionId | string (uuid) |  | The section the item sits in, set by setMenuSections and applyMenuActions (moveSection). (read-only; nullable) |
| isStockTracked | boolean |  | True where a recipe exists. |
| isAvailable | boolean | yes |  |
| unavailableReason | string |  | (nullable) |
| restoreAt | string (date-time) |  | When an unavailable item comes back on its own (setItemAvailability). (nullable) |
| preparationMinutes | integer |  | (nullable) |
| allergens | array of AllergenCode: enum (gluten, crustaceans, eggs, fish, peanuts, soybeans, milk, nuts, …) |  |  |

**Responses**

| Code | Shape | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| 412 | PreconditionFailed | The row changed since the If-Match version was read (SD-013). |
| 400 | BadRequest | Validation failed |
| 200 |  | Updated |
| 429 | TooManyRequests | Rate limit exceeded for this tenant, venue or principal. |

## Group: modifier

### createModifierGroup

**`POST /modifier-groups`**: Create a modifier group

Groups carry selection constraints — minimum, maximum, required. A burger that must have exactly one cooking temperature and may have up to three extras is two groups with different constraints, not free-text.

## Group: order

### createFnbOrder

**`POST /fnb-orders`**: Place an F&B order

Quick service pays immediately; table service attaches to a visit and pays at close.
**When it is paid is the outlet's choice** (Chinmay, 2 October, workbook Q64; refines audit R261 per outlet; CHG-CSA-010). The outlet's `tenancy.Outlet.paymentTiming` (common `OutletPaymentTiming`): `sendFirst`, the default, creates the kitchen tickets with the order and the till charges after (R261); `payFirst` creates the order with no kitchen tickets, and **they are created only when its payment is captured** (`FnbOrder.sentToKitchenAt` stays null until then). An unpaid order at a `payFirst` outlet never reaches the kitchen. The order carries the timing it was placed under (`FnbOrder.paymentTiming`).
Offline behaviour depends on the items: an order containing only untracked items queues normally, while one containing a recipe-linked item is refused offline because stock depletes in real time.

|  |  |
|---|---|
| Permission | `ORDER_CREATE` |
| Scope level | workstation |
| Part of slice | core |
| Wave | 1 |
| Offline | yes |
| Conflict policy | append |
| Reads | `fnb.kitchen_station`, `fnb.kitchen_ticket`, `fnb.kitchen_ticket_line`, `fnb.menu_item`, `fnb.service_order`, `fnb.service_order_line` |
| Writes | `fnb.kitchen_ticket`, `fnb.kitchen_ticket_line`, `fnb.service_order`, `fnb.service_order_line`, `platform.idempotency_record`, `platform.outbox` |
| Called by | EMP-058, EMP-059, POS-022 |
| State model | F&B order ([states/fnb-order.yaml](../../../states/fnb-order.yaml)): created as `ordered`<br/>Table ([states/table.yaml](../../../states/table.yaml)): moves `seated` -> `ordered` |

## Group: production

### setRecipe

**`PUT /recipes`**: Define a recipe for a menu item

**Saving re-runs `verifyAllergens`** for the menu item (decided 28 September, audit R241), and **recomputes `costPerPortion`** from the ingredients (audit R125 (9)). A cost sent in the body is ignored.

|  |  |
|---|---|
| Permission | `PRODUCT_CONFIGURE` |
| Scope level | venue |
| Part of slice | setup, makes `fnb.recipe` non-empty |
| Wave | 1 |
| Offline | no |
| Conflict policy | serverWins |
| Reads | `cache:idempotency`, `fnb.recipe`, `fnb.recipe_ingredient` |
| Writes | `cache:idempotency`, `fnb.recipe`, `fnb.recipe_ingredient` |
| Called by | BO-111 |

**Parameters**

| Name | In | Required | Type | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| If-Match | header |  | string | Optimistic concurrency for serverWins (system-design review SD-013, 29 September; ADR-0031). |
| Idempotency-Key | header | yes | string (uuid) | Client-generated UUIDv7. |

**Request body**: `Recipe`

| Field | Type | Required | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| menuItemId | string (uuid) | yes |  |
| yield | number |  | Portions produced by one execution. (min 0) |
| ingredients | array of object | yes | (min items 1) |
| ingredients[].inventoryItemId | string (uuid) | yes |  |
| ingredients[].quantity | number | yes | (min 0) |
| ingredients[].unit | string | yes |  |
| ingredients[].isOptional | boolean |  | (default False) |
| costPerPortion | object |  | Computed, never entered (decided 28 September, audit R125 (9)): the sum of each ingredient quantity at its current inventory cost, divided by yield. (read-only) |
| costPerPortion.amount | string | yes | Decimal string, never a float. (pattern ^-?\d+(\.\d{1,4})?$) |
| costPerPortion.currency | string | yes | Resolved from the region, not stored on the row (ADR-0018). (pattern ^[A-Z]{3}$) |
| costPerPortion.scale | integer | yes | Resolved from the region alongside currency. (min 0; max 4) |

**Response**: `Recipe`

| Field | Type | Required | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| menuItemId | string (uuid) | yes |  |
| yield | number |  | Portions produced by one execution. (min 0) |

## Group: service

### cancelFnbOrder

**`POST /fnb-orders/{orderId}/cancel`**: Cancel an order

Before acceptance this is a refusal and needs no approval. **After acceptance the kitchen may have started**, so waste is recorded separately and the cancellation needs the same authority as a void.
**Only from `ordered` or `accepted`** (decided 28 September, audit R125 (3)). From `inPreparation` onward the kitchen has the food and the act is a void, with a reason from the void reason list; the refusal names `voidOrder` in `suggestedOperation`.
**An accepted order needs `ORDER_VOID`** (decided 28 September, audit R091 (5)): `ORDER_MODIFY` cancels an order that is still `ordered`, and cancelling one that is `accepted` requires `ORDER_VOID`, the same authority as a void. A caller without it gets a `403` (`void-permission-required`).

|  |  |
|---|---|
| Permission | `ORDER_MODIFY` |
| Scope level | venue |
| Part of slice | core |
| Wave | 1 |
| Offline | yes |
| Conflict policy | serverWins |
| Reads | `cache:idempotency`, `fnb.kitchen_ticket`, `fnb.kitchen_ticket_line`, `fnb.service_order`, `fnb.service_order_line` |
| Writes | `cache:idempotency`, `fnb.service_order` |
| Called by | BO-020, POS-022 |
| State model | F&B order ([states/fnb-order.yaml](../../../states/fnb-order.yaml)): moves `ordered` -> `cancelled`, `accepted` -> `cancelled`<br/>Kitchen ticket ([states/kitchen-ticket.yaml](../../../states/kitchen-ticket.yaml)): moves `received` -> `cancelled`, `preparing` -> `cancelled` |

**Parameters**

| Name | In | Required | Type | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| orderId | path | yes | string (uuid) |  |
| Idempotency-Key | header | yes | string (uuid) | Client-generated UUIDv7. |

**Request body**

| Field | Type | Required | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| recordedAt | string (date-time) | yes | Device time of the act (offline-capable; replayed in this order). |
| reason | enum (outletRefused, guestCancelled, itemUnavailable, tooLong, error) | yes |  |
| note | string |  | (max length 300) |
| recordWaste | boolean |  | Where preparation had started. |

### openTableVisit

**`POST /table-visits`**: Seat a party and open a visit

A visit accumulates orders across the sitting. It is not an order — the guest may order three times and pay once.
Covers is captured at seating because it drives split-by-covers at close.

|  |  |

| Scope level | workstation |
| Part of slice | core |
| Wave | 1 |
| Offline | yes |
| Conflict policy | append |
| Reads | `cache:idempotency`, `fnb.kitchen_ticket`, `fnb.kitchen_ticket_line`, `fnb.service_order`, `fnb.service_order_line`, `fnb.table_visit` |
| Writes | `cache:idempotency`, `fnb.service_order`, `fnb.table_visit` |
| Called by | EMP-058, POS-028 |
| State model | Table visit ([states/table-visit.yaml](../../../states/table-visit.yaml)): created as `open`; moves `billRequested` -> `open` |

### setTableLayout

**`PUT /outlets/{outletId}/tables`**: Configure the table layout

The outlet's whole layout in one call — the outlet is the one in the path. Each table is matched on `TableDefinition.id`: an id already on the outlet updates that table, a new id adds one. A table whose capacity changes is refused while a visit is open on it, as `updateTable` is.
**Nothing is ever deleted** (Chinmay, 2 October, workbook Q67; CHG-CSA-012). "Remove table" on the layout editor marks the table out of service (`TableStatus` `outOfService`), so its history, its visits and its bookings keep their table; a table left out of the body is unchanged, not removed.

|  |  |
|---|---|
| Permission | `PRODUCT_CONFIGURE` |
| Scope level | venue |
| Part of slice | core |
| Wave | 1 |
| Offline | no |
| Config scope | outlet |
| Conflict policy | serverWins |
| Reads | `cache:idempotency`, `fnb.dining_table` |
| Writes | `cache:idempotency`, `fnb.dining_table` |
| Called by | BO-044, EMP-053, POS-024 |
| State model | Table ([states/table.yaml](../../../states/table.yaml)): moves `free` -> `outOfService` |

**Parameters**

| Name | In | Required | Type | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| outletId | path | yes | string (uuid) |  |
| If-Match | header |  | string | Optimistic concurrency for serverWins (system-design review SD-013, 29 September; ADR-0031). |
| Idempotency-Key | header | yes | string (uuid) | Client-generated UUIDv7. |

**Request body**

| Field | Type | Required | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| tables | array of TableDefinition | yes |  |
| tables[].id | string (uuid) | yes |  |
| tables[].label | string | yes | The table code, unique per venue (decided 28 September, audit R108). (max length 32) |
| tables[].capacity | integer | yes | (min 1) |
| tables[].zone | string |  | (nullable) |
| tables[].position | object |  |  |
| tables[].position.x | number |  |  |
| tables[].position.y | number |  |  |
| tables[].shape | enum (round, square, rectangle, booth, bar) |  |  |
| tables[].isOutOfService | boolean |  | Damaged, or its section closed. (default False) |

**Response**: `TableMap`

| Field | Type | Required | Notes |

### updateTableVisit

**`PATCH /table-visits/{visitId}`**: Amend covers, move table, or reassign server

Moving a party to another table is routine and must not lose the visit. The visit is the unit that survives; the table is an attribute of it.
**On the till in Block A: change covers and change server only** (Chinmay, 2 October, workbook Q68, default accepted; POSV2-8; CHG-CSA-013). POS-028 uses this for covers and `transferTableVisit` for the server. Moving the party to another table (and `moveTableVisit`, `mergeTableVisits`) stays on the staff app and comes to the till only after r2.

| Permission | `ORDER_MODIFY` |
| Scope level | venue |
| Part of slice | core |
| Wave | 1 |
| Offline | yes |

| Called by | EMP-058, POS-028 |

## Not in the first release

60 operations, added to this service in later releases without changing any of the above.

| Group | Operations |
|---|---|
| fnb | `applyMenuActions`, `closeCorrectiveAction`, `compItem`, `completeProductionRun`, `createCombo`, `createTable`, `enterCountLine`, `getAllergenVerification`, `getFnbReservationPolicy`, `getFnbServiceChargePolicy`, `getProductionRun`, `listFnbRecommendations`, `listIngredientSubstitutes`, `listProductionRuns`, `listTemperatureCheckpoints`, `moveTableVisit`, `notifyWaitlistParty`, `planProductionRun`, `quoteWaitTime`, `reassignServer`, `recordCorrectiveAction`, `requestRecount`, `resolveBookingConflict`, `sendBookingConfirmation`, `sendOrderNotification`, `setComboSlots`, `setFnbReservationPolicy`, `setFnbServiceChargePolicy`, `setIngredientSubstitutes`, `setSectionLayout`, `setServiceStage`, `setSubstitutionRules`, `setTemperatureCheckpoint`, `transferOrderItems`, `updateTable`, `verifyAllergens` |
| general | `listCombos`, `listSubstitutionRules` |
| guestOrdering | `claimTableSession` |
| kitchen | `getKitchenRoutingRules`, `setKitchenRoutingRules`, `setKitchenStations` |
| menu | `getMenu`, `listMenuSchedules`, `listMenuVersions`, `listMenus`, `setMenuItemDailyCount` |
| modifier | `listModifierGroups` |
| order | `amendFnbOrder` |
| outlet | `listOutletTemplates`, `setOutletTemplate` |
| production | `getPrepSheetTemplate`, `getWasteApprovalPolicy`, `listRecipes`, `printPrepSheet`, `recordWaste`, `setPrepSheetTemplate`, `setWasteApprovalPolicy` |
| service | `acceptFnbOrder` |
| table | `mergeTableVisits` |
